11812 (Oct. 7, 1974, 'shall take effect October 1, 1974'; 39 FR 36307-36312) adjusts for members of the uniformed services the monthly basic pay (Sec. 1 tables), basic allowance for subsistence (Sec. 2) and basic allowance for quarters (Sec. 3), caps basic pay at Executive Schedule level V under 5 U.S.C. 5308 (Sec. 4), supersedes seven earlier uniformed-pay orders (Sec. 5) and sets its effective date (Sec. 6). It has no term of its own; its rates govern until the next adjustment. EO 11883 (signed Oct. 6, 1975) sec. 5 supersedes 'Executive Order No. 11812 of October 7, 1974', and its sec. 2 replacement rates for basic pay, subsistence and quarters 'shall be effective on October 1, 1975' (sec. 4). Every clause except Sec. 5 therefore first ended on 1975-10-01, the stated effective date in the acting instrument (the displacement was retroactive from that date to Oct. 6). Sec. 5's supersession of earlier orders was completed when the order issued on 1974-10-07 (spent). Whole-order end = 1975-10-01. All of this order concerns the uniformed services, so the agency-specific civilian pay-period dates in EO 11883 sec. 4 do not affect it. Counterevidence: the U.S. Code note under 37 U.S.C. 1009 states that EO 11812 'which provided for the adjustment of pay rates effective Oct. 1, 1974, was superseded by Ex. Ord. No. 11883' (history, not a predicate); the 1989 Codification row reads 'Superseded by EO 11883'. CONFLICT QUARANTINED_RELATIONSHIP_CONFLICT resolved: the quarantined relationship (research/stage15/AUDIT/RELATIONSHIP_CHRONOLOGY_VIOLATIONS.csv) is an OFR note on EO 11778 (Apr. 12, 1974) reading 'Amends: EO 11692, December 15, 1972 (which was revoked by EO 11812, October 7, 1974)'; the parenthetical names this order as the later superseder of EO 11692, it is not an event on this order, and the Stage 15 audit already suppressed it. The draft had no clause inventory; it is built here from the official FR scan.","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11812#enacting"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11812#sec1"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11812#sec2"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11812#sec3"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11812#sec4"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11812#sec5"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11812#sec6"}],"source_pins":[]}]}

The Committee (ten labor and ten management members appointed by the President) was created April 1, 1975, after the Federal Advisory Committee Act took effect; under its sec. 14(a)(2) (held 86 Stat. 776) such a committee terminates not later than two years after establishment unless renewed. EO 11948 (effective 1976-12-31), the general continuance order, did not renew it, and EO 12110 sec. 1-103(b) describes it as terminated or its work completed. Whether the Act applied and whether any separate renewal was made has not been established, so the expiry date (about April 1, 1977) is not asserted.","whole_order_end not set until the first-end question is resolved (latest possible date 1978-12-31, EO 12110)."],"dossier_fingerprint":"fe75de053c0ff171bb3d480acad8236b990a7fda33e91e7de0c2ef68adcc2c1e","existing_lifecycle_questions":[{"clause_sha256":"ec17bca3c1fb74a94af5b722d9cced550565dfb8ec7ac046166b8ecc6ef88d7d","dossier_pointer":"/lifecycle_obligations/0","later_actor_evidence_ids":["ACT:EO:11849>EO:12110"],"own_term_evidence_ids":[],"questions":["Determine the first cessation or current predicate for this scope; end of every clause not established. The Committee (ten labor and ten management members appointed by the President) was created April 1, 1975, after the Federal Advisory Committee Act took effect; under its sec. 14(a)(2) (held 86 Stat. 776) such a committee terminates not later than two years after establishment unless renewed. EO 11948 (effective 1976-12-31), the general continuance order, did not renew it, and EO 12110 sec. 1-103(b) describes it as terminated or its work completed. Whether the Act applied and whether any separate renewal was made has not been established, so the expiry date (about April 1, 1977) is not asserted.","whole_order_end not set until the first-end question is resolved (latest possible date 1978-12-31, EO 12110)."],"research_rationale":"EO 11849 (April 1, 1975; 40 FR 14887-14888, read in the held govinfo scan) created the Collective Bargaining Committee in Construction (secs. 1-6). The order has no term of its own. EO 12110 sec. 1-103(b) revoked it effective December 31, 1978 as one whose committee had 'terminated' or whose work was 'completed', which signals an earlier end. The likely earlier end is the two-year limit of Federal Advisory Committee Act sec. 14(a)(2), since the general continuance order of December 1976 (EO 11948) did not renew it. Because the Act's application and the absence of any other renewal are not proven from held sources, first ends and the whole-order end are left open. Savings: none found. The U.S. Code note (29 U.S.C. 171 note) and the eCFR hits are historical and report the revocation; no CONFLICT is open.","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec1"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec2a"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec2b"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec2c"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec3-a"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec3-b"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec3-c"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec3-d"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec3-e"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec3-f"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec3-g"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec4"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11849#sec5"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","differ It sets the base production control level for marginal oil properties. Inventory: three numbered sections (1-101 to 1-103), recorded as one whole clause; the dossier inventory matches the full held text. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, Federal Register of March 3, 1986; held government printing) revokes the orders listed in its table, and any amendments thereto; its enacting provision has no stated effective date, so the first end is dated on signing, 1986-02-25 (a reading, disclosed for the reviewer). The Federal Register publication and filing dates are not effective dates. The table lists 12187, whose subject line describes providing for the base production control level for marginal properties. Executive Order 12209 (1980-04-16) restated the order and is a later event, not an end. Fixed period of the restated section 1-101: Executive Order 12209, signed April 16, 1980, makes section 1-101 apply only with respect to the months of January, February and March 1980, and says it was issued to permit the conversion to new oil status of all old oil production from marginal oil wells effective April 1, 1980. That fixed period ended on March 31, 1980 (inclusive; the exclusive reading would end it when that day begins), before the restatement was signed, so the pricing rule has no application to any later month and the order was functionally spent as to that rule on March 31, 1980. The restatement itself took effect on signing, April 16, 1980 (no effective date is stated). This own-terms period is a disclosed earlier candidate end and is not adopted as the first end: the restatement leaves section 1-102 (the definition of marginal properties) and section 1-103 (the Secretary of Energy's power to adopt conforming regulations) in force without any date, the restated section 1-101 still fixes the base production control level for the covered months, and under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on strike-and-insert rewrites the clause continues, amended, until the parent order ends, which here is the express revocation by Executive Order 12553.  The packet’s number-citation lookup over the held order texts (33,118 readable files) found citations of this number outside its own printings only in the printings of EO 12209. Searches: the OFR executive-order catalog search and the USC and eCFR citation searches were selected after reading their coverage notes (complete; no coverage warning; the USC edition runs through 2026-10-09 and the eCFR search to 2026-10-14, which cannot matter for an order whose end is shown in 1986 or earlier).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) Executive Order 12209 amended and limited the order to three months of 1980; under owner ruling 2026-10-21 that is an amendment (later event), not a revocation. (2) Fixed period of the restated section 1-101: Executive Order 12209, signed April 16, 1980, makes section 1-101 apply only with respect to the months of January, February and March 1980, and says it was issued to permit the conversion to new oil status of all old oil production from marginal oil wells effective April 1, 1980. That fixed period ended on March 31, 1980 (inclusive; the exclusive reading would end it when that day begins), before the restatement was signed, so the pricing rule has no application to any later month and the order was functionally spent as to that rule on March 31, 1980. The restatement itself took effect on signing, April 16, 1980 (no effective date is stated). This own-terms period is a disclosed earlier candidate end and is not adopted as the first end: the restatement leaves section 1-102 (the definition of marginal properties) and section 1-103 (the Secretary of Energy's power to adopt conforming regulations) in force without any date, the restated section 1-101 still fixes the base production control level for the covered months, and under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on strike-and-insert rewrites the clause continues, amended, until the parent order ends, which here is the express revocation by Executive Order 12553. (3) Whether the authority of the Emergency Petroleum Allocation Act of 1973 lapsed before 1986 is not established from held sources (the statute text is not held), and such a lapse would be an unnamed instrument and a disclosed candidate only. Executive Order 12287 of January 28, 1981 (held Federal Register text jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12287.txt; not among this packet’s citable evidence) exempted all crude oil and refined petroleum products from the price and allocation controls adopted under that Act without naming this order; it is likewise a disclosed candidate earlier end and not an end, and it is not recorded as a later event. No held order revokes or supersedes this order before EO 12553. ## Valla review leads and component attestations

Review lead only. Every case needs separate applicability, earlier-ending, scope, independent-command, savings and independent whole-case review.
Preparation attestations never approve legal conclusions or replace whole-case review.
